PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended March 81, 1898.
184 CUSTOMSGAZErTE. JAN.-MAR NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 317. YANGTZE RIVER-KIUKIANG DISTRICT. TWO-FATHOM CREEK BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Two-fathom Creek Beacon Light has been shifted 7oo yards N. 520 W. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank SHANGHAI, I5th January 1898. No. 318. KIUNGCHOW DISTRICT. BUOYAGE OF HAINAN STRAIT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Hainan Head Bank Buoy has been shifted about 2 cables to the south-westward of its former position, on account of the extension of the bank in that direction; and that it is now moored in a5 feet at low water of spring tides, with the summit of Hainan Bluff bearing about S. 650 W., magnetic, and distant about 174 cables. DIRECTIONs. With the Hainan Head Bank Buoy in the position above described, and the Riversdale Rock and the Hainan Reef Buoys in their advertised positions, vessels entering the South Channel from the eastward should pass, say, 2 cables N.E. of the Riversdale Rock Buoy, and then steer to pass, say, I cable S.W. of the Hainan Head Bank Buoy, taking care to guard against being set towards the shore, and remembering that it is safe to borrow towards the banks on the starboard hand, of which the lead, which should be kept going, will give warning, while on the other side the bottom&#39; is rocky and uneven and the lead gives no warning. The Hainan Reef Buoy may be rounded at a distance of I cable or more at discretion. SHANGHAI, 14th February 1898.

APPENDIX I. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JANUARY-MARCH 1898.
198 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. JAN.-MAR., (Received 2ist April 1898.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 14TH APRIL 1898. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the first quarter of the year 1898, January-March. b. The Custom House (A i| U) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two, Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty And Convention Likin on Opium (H1k.yh 3o and Hk.M 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (I * ), Changehow ( g), Fotochow ( j), and Kowloon (A 5) stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these tatistics means to China, antl the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 1898.] LAPA. 209 (Received 21st April 1898.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 12TH APRIL 1898. I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the first quarter of the year I898, January-March. b. The Custom House (9 ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The oWction of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.7&amp;amp; 3o and Hk.Yh 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow ( F1 ivl) and Chienshan (j [Il) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (5 A), Kuttai (W *), and Kwanchiap (U ) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
